## 2026-08-19 (cycle 1182) — Phyllotaxis as GROWTH, not pattern: a recording that seeds itself into a Fibonacci bloom (+ the WIDE spread)

**Dive.** JURY 2026-08-18's two named ceiling targets are both now extended — `pulse`→`15248-coherence` (§1180, breath as 2nd biosignal) and `answerback`→`15312-transcript` (§1181, retrieval as a conversation). With the "extend a 4/5" directive satisfied and three DEEPs run back-to-back (1179/1180/1181), the alternation calls for **WIDE**. Scanned recent audio-visual / generative-design work for a fresh technique that is NOT a physics/agent simulation (the jury's still-banned meta-pattern, 7×) and NOT on the over-used surfaces (raw-WebGL2 4×, cool-teal palette 4×).

- **Fresh dated source — "Music Visualization Using Dynamic Phyllotactic Patterns: A Generative Design Approach with Bézier Curve Mapping" (Archives of Design Research, 2026; aodr.org/xml/48501/48501.pdf).** The surprising, load-bearing claim: **phyllotaxis is not a static pattern but the product of dynamic GROWTH** — primordia emitted one at a time at the meristem and pushed outward by spatial competition (Vogel's `angle = i·137.507°`, `radius = c·√i`). Most generative phyllotaxis art seeds a *fixed* field and then modulates it; the paper's move is to treat the golden-angle emission itself as the music's timeline, and to smooth the inter-seed trajectory with Bézier curves. Why it matters for the lab: a recording can *grow itself* into a seed-head — each note it plays places the next seed — so the finished bloom is a readable record of the whole piece (21/34 Fibonacci parastichies emerging from the music's own note density), not an FFT snapshot. This is generative-deterministic GROWTH, categorically not a physics/agent sim → dodges the jury's banned meta-pattern while giving the lab its rare full-chromatic register (chroma→hue).
- **Honesty check (grep).** Phyllotaxis is NOT a lab-first: `3424-attending` already seats ~37 synth voice-glyphs on a *static* Vogel spiral (mic-presence, inline-SVG, pre-rule-10). So the fresh, defensible move is not the golden-angle packing — it is **real-catalog-DRIVEN growth/accretion** (note-timed seed emission from `loadTrackAnalysis().notes[]`) + Bézier melodic-contour spokes + chroma-pitch-class hue readback. Clear the floor via #2 (≥3 subsystems) + #3 (named ref: the 2026 paper + Vogel 1979) + #5 (this dive), not #1.
- **The WIDE spread (3 divergent non-sim briefs, each dodging every ban — raw-WebGL2 / inline-SVG / cool-teal / physics-sim):**
  1. **`15328-bloomwork`** — the research hook. three.js · full-chromatic · phyllotactic real-catalog GROWTH · passive catalog+selector input.
  2. **`15344-toll`** — the jury's most-repeated 0×-in-window ask (conceptual/critical: "a recording you can't hear without paying a cost"). DOM/type + minimal Canvas2D · achromatic ink+one-red · subtractive/irreversible listening (spending a phrase consumes it forever) · discrete-spend input. Ref: Yoko Ono *Cut Piece* (1964) — the audience subtracts from the artwork. The inverse of `3424-attending` (which "subtracts nothing") and the subtractive sibling of `14848-holdlight`.
  3. **`15360-sediment`** — the long-form/stateful lane. Canvas2D isometric · warm stone/amber (a register absent from the recent window) · spectral **sedimentation** (each moment deposits a stratum; the core at minute 5 is a thick record of everything played, not a loop) · pointer-orbit + tilt. Ref: Ryoji Ikeda *data.matrix* / stratigraphy.
- **Could-become-a-prototype (future):** a phyllotaxis bloom seeded by TWO tracks interleaved (cross-album parastichies); a subtractive piece where spent phrases can be *gifted* to a second listener (multi-user cost); a sediment core you can physically "drill" to resurface a buried motif.
- **Chain:** today's research (phyllotactic growth) → today's WIDE build, winner curated from the 3 (case a for `bloomwork`; b/c honestly documented for the other two).

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1181) — Cross-catalog retrieval as a real two-way CONVERSATION: the catalog answering itself

**Dive.** Following JURY 2026-08-18 (the constraint is the *ceiling*; a third 4/5 piece beats a fourteenth 3/5; extend `pulse` or `answerback` — `pulse`'s breath extension shipped as §1180, so `answerback` is the remaining named DEEP target: "deepen the cross-catalog retrieval into a real two-way conversation"). Scanned `arxiv.org/list/cs.SD/2026-08` for where music retrieval / musical dialogue is at the front.

- **Fresh dated source — arXiv:2608.04543 (Aug 2026), "Towards Robust Version Identification in the Wild: A Dataset, Benchmark, and Fine-Tuning Study."** The 2026 retrieval frontier is **robust cross-catalog musical matching** — identifying the same underlying musical work across a catalog despite surface differences (arrangement, key, timbre, live vs. studio). Why it matters for the build: `14656-answerback` already does a lightweight, artistic version of exactly this verb (match a live phrase's *shape* against phrases sliced from across his catalog and answer with the real audio), but only as a **single** call→response. The field's push toward robust cross-catalog matching says the interesting object is not one match — it's the *graph* of relatedness across a whole body of work. Turn the single hop into a **walk**: a self-continuing chain where each retrieved phrase seeds the next retrieval, and the conversation traverses all 16 tracks.
- **Contrast source — arXiv:2606.11886 (2026), "Real-Time Language Model Jamming: A Case Study for Live Music Accompaniment Generation"** (and the older RL-Duet, arXiv:2002.03082, 2020). The live-jamming / duet frontier is dominated by models that **synthesize** the partner's part in real time. That is the opposite of the lab's standing contract (rule 10: every sound is Karel's real recording, arranged by no one). So the apt, differentiated move is an **anti-synthesis** conversation: a duet where *every* turn — both "speakers" — is a real recorded phrase retrieved from his catalog, not a generated note. The machine never plays; it only *quotes* him back to himself.
- **Supporting refs (foundational).** Query-by-humming / query-by-contour MIR retrieval; Paul Lamere's **Infinite Jukebox (2012)** — navigating *one* recording by self-similarity. answerback moved that from within-song to across-catalog; this cycle moves it from one exchange to a self-sustaining, harmonically-aware conversation.
- **The apt move for the lab.** answerback (§, 4/5) is, per the jury, "the only piece here with a verb that isn't conduct/mix/transform/simulate." Deepen it three ways it never had: (1) **memory** — each answer's contour + implied harmony seeds the next query, so the catalog answers its own answer (a chain, not a single reply); (2) **harmonic answering** — score against the chord/key track, not melody alone (answerback's own listed next-idea); (3) **whole-catalog traversal** — an anti-latch bias pulls the conversation toward tracks it has visited least, so the whole body of work is drawn into one thread. It is pure retrieval/composition (dodges the jury's banned "recording drives a physics/agent SIMULATION"), achromatic (the jury's rare-healthy palette), and self-propelling so it fully demos headless (no MIDI/mic needed) — directly addressing the standing "everything is headless-blind" unblock. Rule 10 clean by construction: zero synthesis, every turn a real decoded slice through one `createSafeMaster`.
- **→ became cycle 1181's DEEP build** ("the catalog in conversation with itself," ONE concept raced via 2 surfaces): **`conversation`** — three.js achromatic "catalog constellation" (16 tracks as silver nodes; the conversation a luminous thread hopping node→node, each node brightening as it's drawn in); vs **`transcript`** — DOM/type "living transcript" (a scrolling chat log whose speakers are his own recordings; each turn a mono line + a tiny grayscale contour sparkline). Shipped the stronger; banked the other. Chain: today's research → today's build (case a).
- **Could-become-a-prototype (future):** a **branching** conversation (each turn offers 2–3 candidate answers as forks you can steer down — the retrieval graph made navigable, closer to the version-ID *graph* the fresh source is really about); or **two catalogs in dialogue** (his Welcome Home "answering" his Snowflake, so the conversation is cross-*album*); or DTW-aligned contour matching to reward ornamented local alignment.

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1180) — Respiratory COHERENCE as a biofeedback carrier: breathe his recording out of a veil

**Dive.** Following JURY 2026-08-18 (extend the 4/5 `15152-pulse` with the breath-coherence second biosignal it named), scanned current HRV-biofeedback literature for what a browser can honestly reward. The load-bearing concept is **resonance-frequency breathing** (~0.1 Hz, ~6 breaths·min⁻¹): slow paced breathing at that rate maximally stimulates the baroreflex and phase-locks cardiac + respiratory rhythms into large coherent oscillations (Lehrer & Vaschillo, the canon of resonance-frequency HRV biofeedback).

- **Fresh dated source — Scientific Reports 2026 RCT.** "Resonance frequency versus fixed 0.1 Hz breathing in HRV biofeedback: a four-week randomized comparison" (nature.com/articles/s41598-026-53333-6). Four-week RCT, three arms (individually-tuned RF vs fixed 0.1 Hz vs control): both breathing arms significantly reduced stress/anxiety/depressive symptoms vs control, with **no meaningful difference between individualized RF and a fixed 0.1 Hz pace** (Bayesian anecdotal evidence for the null). Why it matters for the build: I can pace confidently at a **fixed 0.1 Hz** and reward **phase-locking to the pacer** (coherence) as the mechanism — no per-user resonance-frequency calibration step needed, which would be infeasible headless/first-visit anyway.
- **Supporting ref.** "Guiding Breathing at the Resonance Frequency with Haptic Sensors Potentiates Cardiac Coherence" (*Sensors* 2023, doi 10.3390/s23094494) — guiding breath to RF raises measured cardiac coherence; the guide modality (here, a slow luminance/aperture pacer) matters.
- **The apt move for the lab.** `pulse` (§1178, 4/5) read the visitor's *heartbeat* (camera PPG) and entrained his bass to it — the lab's first biosignal piece. The obvious, jury-named deepening is the **second** biosignal, **breath**, with a *reward loop* the lab has never had: don't just react to a body signal, reward the visitor for *entraining* to a target. Coherence = a 0..1 score (phase-locking value of breath-vs-pacer + rate-match to 10 s) that **lifts one real take out of a veil** (muffled+distant → clear+present) as the visitor's breathing locks to the pace. Rule 10 clean by construction — the only audible sound is his recording; the breath + pacer are control-only, the mic dead-ends at an analyser and is never routed to output.
- **→ became cycle 1180's DEEP build** ("breathe his recording out of a veil," ONE concept raced via 2 approaches): **`coherence`** — WebGL2 achromatic "breath lens" (a caustic that focuses to a still point as coherence→1; veil-lift = lowpass 320 Hz→18 kHz + gain + receding delay tail); vs **`tidebreath`** — Canvas2D "tide/aperture to light" (aperture dilates on the inhale as the breathing guide; "approach from far" wet/dry distance chain). Shipped `coherence` (stronger surface diversity + surprise + robustness); banked `tidebreath` IDEAS §1180. Chain: today's research → today's build (case a).
- **Could-become-a-prototype (future):** close the loop back to `pulse` — a **combined breath+heartbeat coherence** piece that scores *RSA* (respiratory sinus arrhythmia: does the camera-PPG heart rate rise on the inhale and fall on the exhale?), the actual physiological signature the RF-breathing literature is about — the lab's first *two-biosignal* interoceptive piece. Or a **breath-conducted duet**: coherence gates which of two real takes is present, so slow breathing crossfades his catalog.

# Resonance Dream — Research Log

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1179) — Making the invisible medium visible: sound as disturbed AIR (synthetic schlieren)

**Dive.** Scanned the last-30-days WebGPU/creative-coding showcase + `arxiv.org/list/cs.SD/2026-08` front for a technique the 1,178-piece lab has genuinely never used. The fresh-front signal was **GPU-compute audio-reactive physics fields**: Ash Weeks' fixed-timestep + GPU-interpolation audio-reactive rigid-body demo and Robert Borghesi's *ASTRODITHER* (audio-reactive three.js **WebGPU/TSL** fluid + dithering), both surfaced on the WebGPU community showcase (2026) — the browser is now a real-time GPU field solver, and every recent showcase piece is *some* audio-forced ping-pong field rendered through *some* optics. That last clause is the opening: the lab has done many audio-forced fields (FDTD wave `15104`, Gray-Scott `reefmind`, physarum `15040`) but always rendered them **literally** (the field IS the image). No piece has rendered a field through a *named optical instrument*.

- **The never-used technique — Schlieren / shadowgraph imaging.** Toepler (1864) invented schlieren; A.J. Settles' *Schlieren and Shadowgraph Techniques* (Springer, 2001) is the canon. Schlieren makes invisible **density / refractive-index gradients** in a transparent medium visible — it is literally how shockwaves, heat plumes, and **sound waves in air** are photographed. Two flavors, both 0× in the lab (grep INDEX: `schlieren` = 0, `shadowgraph` = 0, `diffraction` = 0): (1) **classic knife-edge schlieren** — a knife-edge at the focal point cuts one sign of the ray deflection, so ∇ρ projected on the knife direction maps to a luminous grayscale image around mid-gray; (2) **Background-Oriented Schlieren (BOS)** — Dalziel et al.'s *synthetic schlieren* / Raffel's BOS review: infer the gradient field from the **apparent displacement of a known background pattern** (a dot field / starfield) seen through the disturbed medium, i.e. `uv' = uv + k·∇density`.
- **Why it's the surprising, apt move.** Sound *is* a pressure disturbance of air — schlieren is the one imaging method whose literal purpose is to photograph exactly that. So "see your recording as the air it moves" is not a metaphor stretched over a fader bank; it is the physically correct picture of what a piano in a room actually does to the medium. It is a **room, not a mixer** (jury's standing demand): ONE source (one real take) forcing a density field you watch billow, with no track-gains to set. And it lands in the **achromatic / grayscale register** the jury flagged as the rare *healthy* palette (knife-edge schlieren is intrinsically monochrome; BOS is cool-white stars on black). Rule 10 clean by construction — the only audible sound is his recording; the field is a control-only visualization.
- **→ became cycle 1179's DEEP build** ("see Karel's recording as disturbed air," raced via 2 optical models on ONE concept): **`schlieren`** — a WebGL2 ping-pong density field forced by his take's band-energy/onsets, rendered as a classic **knife-edge grayscale shadowgraph** (∇ρ · knife-edge → luminous plumes; pointer rotates the knife-edge, a real schlieren control); vs **`shadowgraph`** — the same audio-forced field rendered as **Background-Oriented Schlieren**, a dim procedural **starfield refracted** through the medium (`uv+k·∇density`) so the stars shimmer and lens where his music disturbs the air — a cosmic heat-haze. Shipped the stronger; banked the other IDEAS §1179. Chain: today's research → today's build (case a).
- **Could-become-a-prototype (future):** a *true* BOS pipeline that runs on a live camera feed (front camera as the background, your breath/voice near the mic as the disturbance) — but rule 10 keeps that secondary; the honest next step is a **3D volumetric schlieren** (raymarch the density volume, integrate ∇n along each ray) so his recording becomes a plume you orbit, not a flat field — the lab's first raymarched-volume optics.

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1178) — Interoception as the instrument: your pulse and your stillness as the SECONDARY control over one real take

**Dive.** Scanned the last-30-days interoception / altered-states-through-breath-and-body pulse (the PRIMARY DIRECTION is altered states through light + sound; the jury's starved lane is the cosmic-ambient / meditative pole). Fresh 2026 work converges on one idea: the body's own interoceptive signals — heartbeat, breath, vagal tone — are a *route into* altered/meditative states, and sound can be tuned to them in real time.

- **Sources (all 2025–2026).** *Heartbeat Resonance: Inducing Non-contact Heartbeat Sensations in the Chest* (ACM CHI 2025, doi:10.1145/3706598.3713959) — low-frequency sound reproduces a felt heartbeat in the chest cavity, ~78 Hz most effective; a felt cardiac pulse can be *induced* by sound alone. *Sensory processing reallocation from auditory to cardiac signals in REM sleep* (Current Biology, 2026, S0960-9822(26)00889-4) — in dream sleep the brain reweights attention from the ears toward the heartbeat, i.e. the heartbeat *becomes* the perceptual foreground in dream states. *Neurobiological substrates of altered states induced by high-ventilation breathwork accompanied by music* (PLOS One / PMC12385377, 2026) and the *Airways to Alteration (A2A)* breathwork trial (Frontiers in Psychology, 2026) — the depth of the altered state tracks cardiovascular + interoceptive activation, and **music is the paired carrier**. Foundational anchor: Pauline Oliveros, *Deep Listening* — attention to the body and the room as the practice `[older, foundational]`.
- **Why it's surprising / the reframe.** ~All 1,177 lab pieces read the *external* signal — the recording's FFT/onsets/chords, or an external sensor (sun, tilt, pose). **Zero read the visitor's own body's interoceptive signals.** The reframe: make the visitor's *interior state* the secondary control layer over ONE of Karel's real takes (rule 10 — his catalog is the only audible source; the body only *shapes* it, never replaces it). Two bodily routes, both 0× in the lab: (1) **the heartbeat, read optically** — a fingertip on the phone camera (torch on) gives a photoplethysmography (PPG) signal in the red-channel brightness oscillation; detect BPM and let his recording **entrain** to it (swell + low-shelf throb on the down-beat of your own pulse — the CHI "felt heartbeat" done with his music, not a synth tone), so in REM-like fashion the heartbeat pulls the music into its own time; (2) **stillness, read as the absence of motion** — front-camera optical-flow magnitude (or pointer velocity) as an agitation signal that *veils* the take when you fidget and *clears* it when you go still, rewarding ~6-breath/min calm.
- **Could become a prototype that does X.** (a) `pulse` — **the first biosignal piece in the lab**: camera-PPG heart-rate → entrain the swell/low-shelf of one real take to your own pulse + a felt ~sub-bass throb carved from HIS recording (0 synth), a cardiac-waveform readout; synthetic ~62 bpm fallback so a fingerless/cameraless review still beats. (b) `stillroom` — motion-as-veil meditative clear-field: hold still and one time-stretched real take clears from haze to presence; front-camera optical-flow (secondary) makes it *bodily* stillness, pointer-velocity fallback. Both fill the starved meditative pole; (a) opens a genuinely new lab category (interoceptive/biofeedback input). Chain: today's research → today's build (case a) for the WIDE winner.

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1177) — A room, not a mixer, taken literally: wave-based auralization as the anti-fader

**Dive.** Scanned `arxiv.org/list/cs.SD/2026-08` (last-30-days front) + the room-acoustics / auralization pulse. The jury's headline for two verdicts running is *"give me a room, not a mixer,"* and the lab's diagnosed rut is *"controller-X drives the 16-track fader bank."* Today's reframe reads that line **literally**: build an actual *acoustic room* and put one recording inside it.

- **The technique — wave-based room auralization via FDTD.** Savioja, *"Real-Time 3D Finite-Difference Time-Domain Simulation of Low- and Mid-Frequency Room Acoustics"* (DAFx 2010), and the GPGPU room-acoustics line that followed (CUDA FDTD/K-DWM room solvers; "wave-based auralization offers a complete solution to room acoustics, capturing diffraction, occlusion and modal standing waves that geometric/ray methods miss at low frequency"). Complement: the **image-source method**, Allen & Berkley, *"Image method for efficiently simulating small-room acoustics"* (JASA 1979) — mirror the source across each wall to get exact early reflections. Both are **foundational, well-understood, and have NEVER appeared in the 1,176-prototype lab** (grep INDEX/READMEs: 0 wave-equation sims, 0 image-source acoustics; the closest is `14896-chladnichord`'s *static* Chladni nodal figures, which are a plate eigenmode lookup, not a propagating wave sim).
- **Why it's the surprising move / the reframe.** Every "room" the lab has shipped is a metaphor — a fader bank wearing a costume (sun/clock/heading/body/tilt/field → 16 gains). A **real wave field** is not a mixer at all: there is ONE source (one take), and its sound *propagates, reflects, and interferes into standing waves* the visitor physically walks between — antinodes loud, nodes silent. You don't set gains; you occupy an acoustic space and the space decides what you hear at your position. It is the jury's line made physical, and it lands the lab's first genuine **wave-propagation** technique.
- **→ became cycle 1177's DEEP build** (raced 2 approaches on ONE concept "walk inside the resonant wave-field of one recording"): a raw-WebGL2 **2D FDTD pressure field** (top-down, listener samples the local field for spatialized gain + distance delay/lowpass; standing-wave nodes/antinodes are the instrument) vs a three.js **image-source echo chamber** (first-person room, position-dependent early-reflection tap bank). Both rule-10-clean (one real take through one `createSafeMaster`, 0 synth; the wave field / room geometry is the control layer). Shipped the stronger; banked the other IDEAS §1177. Chain: today's research → today's build (case a).
- **Could-become-a-prototype (future):** a *modifiable* room — drag the walls / open a window and hear the modal structure and reverberation change in real time (interactive-auralization line); or seed multiple recordings at different source points so the room's interference braids the catalog spatially (still not a fader — the mix is done by physics, by where you stand).


## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1176) — Emergent transport networks & networked sensorial sound art: the recording as an organism, not a mix

**Dive.** Scanned `arxiv.org/list/cs.SD/2026-08` (last-30-days front) plus the creative-coding/WebGPU pulse. Two live-front signals converged into today's build:
- **arXiv:2608.02219 — "Sounding Canvas: Embedding Algorithms in Networked, Sensorial Sound Art"** (cs.SD, Aug 2026). Argues for sound art where a *running algorithm* — not a fixed mix or timeline — is the artwork, and the sensorial surface is emergent. Read alongside **arXiv:2608.03040 "Calliphony"** (real-time generative gesture→music) and the broader 2026 WebGPU-compute wave (Codrops 2025-in-review + "Beyond WebGL: real-time fluid sims with WebGPU compute shaders," 2026): physarum/slime-mold, reaction-diffusion, Turing patterns, and agent swarms are now interactive-framerate in-browser via WGSL compute.
- **The reframe (why it's surprising for THIS lab):** the jury's headline is "give me a room, not a mixer" and the lab's rut is "controller-X drives the 16-track fader bank." An **emergent transport network** is the antithesis: a swarm of trivial agents with no leader and no fader builds an efficient network by depositing and following a chemical trail (Jones 2010's reduction of *Physarum polycephalum*). If the music shapes the swarm's local rules — sensor angle, deposit strength, where attractor nodes seed — the emergent web becomes a *live portrait of the recording's form growing over its duration*, arranged by no one. It carries the one contract the jury actually praised (reefmind, "arranged by no one, emergent") from a reaction-diffusion field into an agent-based transport network, and does it on **WebGPU compute** — the freshest, least-worn surface available (dodges the Canvas2D ban, the crowded raw-WebGL2/inline-SVG/three.js).
- **→ became `15040-slimenerve`** (WIDE winner). The two runners-up chained to adjacent references on the same "reveal structure, don't mix" axis: **Foote 1999** self-similarity (→ `15056-recurrence`, an audible recurrence plot) and **Pauline Oliveros *Deep Listening*** (→ `15072-stillness`, attention-as-instrument). Both banked IDEAS §1176.
- **Could-become-a-prototype (future):** the "Sounding Canvas" networked angle points at a *multi-visitor* physarum where each present tab seeds its own attractor colony into a shared trail-map (BroadcastChannel) — the swarm braids the room's several recordings into one network. A natural `slimenerve` v2 that also cashes the multi-user lane.

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1175) — The leaderless room: co-presence that self-synchronizes with no conductor

**Source:** the ISMIR-2026 line on **human-to-human musical co-presence as a communication problem** — *H2H Music Improv: A Communication Model and Audio-Visual Dataset for Music Improvisation* (arXiv, cs.SD Aug 2026), which models improvising musicians as agents exchanging minimal signals to stay together without a score or a leader. Framed against the classic decentralized-synchronization literature it descends from: **Kuramoto's coupled-oscillator model (1975)**, **Huygens' 1665 "sympathy of clocks"** (two pendulum clocks on a shared beam entrain), and **Strogatz, *Sync* (2003)** `[older, foundational]`, plus the process-music tradition — **Terry Riley, *In C* (1964)** (53 modules, each player advances at will but stays near the others) and **Steve Reich**'s phase music.

**Why it's the surprising move / the reframe.** The jury's single loudest, most-repeated, still-unmet demand is *"give me a room, not a mixer" / a genuinely multi-user piece* — **0 in the last 15**, flagged thin for 15+ cycles (provocations #1/#2). The lab's one prior multi-user-on-his-catalog piece, `13568-canoncircle` (cycle 1145, ~23 cycles stale), builds the round with a **leader-elected hard shared clock** — one tab is elected conductor and imposes the grid on everyone. The H2H framing + Kuramoto point at the *opposite, richer* architecture: **no conductor at all.** Each present voice is a phase oscillator that only weakly pulls toward what it hears from the others; the round **emerges** from decentralized mutual influence and can fall in and out of lock (fireflies, Huygens' clocks). That directly extends the one contract the jury praised in `14576-reefmind` — *arranged by no one, emergent* — into a **social** setting: the composition is assembled by the room's mutual listening, not delivered by a mixer or dictated by a leader.

**Could become a prototype that does X:** raced this cycle (DEEP) as two decentralized-sync engines over Karel's real catalog — **`15008-sympathy`** (Kuramoto/Huygens phase-coupling: each voice pulls toward the group's mean phase, a coherence order-parameter *r* rising 0→1 as they entrain; raw-WebGL2 oscillator-field) vs **`15024-incommon`** (*In C* additive process: an ordered sequence of catalog phrase-modules, each player advancing at its own pace but consensus-constrained to stay near the group's spread; inline-SVG module-ladder). Both leaderless, both rule-10-clean (every sound = a real take through one `createSafeMaster`, 0 synth), both with **ghost participants** so a solo tab still hears/sees the full emergent ensemble and yields when real peers arrive. **Honest novelty:** multi-user-canon-on-his-catalog is NOT a lab-first (canoncircle 1145); the fresh move is *leaderless/emergent* sync (vs leader-clock) + the reefmind no-conductor contract carried into co-presence. Case a — today's research → today's build.

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1174) — The melody your two ears assemble that is in neither of them: dichotic illusion

**Source:** Diana Deutsch's **scale illusion** (1973) and her **dichotic / phantom-word** research, surveyed live this cycle via her *Musical Illusions and Phantom Words* (Oxford Univ. Press) and current 2026 work extending it — e.g. *Modifying Deutsch's scale illusion for application in music* (PMC9891530), which reconstructs familiar melodies ("Lightly Row", "Jingle Bells") from *jagged dichotic tone patterns* the ears never physically hear as tunes, and 2026 arXiv work using audio illusions as LLM-hard CAPTCHAs (2601.08516) — evidence the phenomenon is a live research front, not a curio. Lineage: **Albert Bregman, *Auditory Scene Analysis* (1990)** `[older, foundational]` — the brain groups sound by pitch-proximity / good-continuation, not by which ear it arrived in.

**Why it's the surprising move / the reframe.** Nearly every one of the lab's ~1,100 pieces treats the audio→visual map as a function of the *physical signal* — FFT bins, RMS, onsets, chords. Deutsch's dichotic illusions prove the physical and the *perceived* signal can DIVERGE completely: split successive notes hard L/R by pitch height and each ear physically receives a jagged, meaningless jump-sequence, yet the listener hears a single smooth phantom contour that exists in **neither channel**. The composition is assembled by the auditory cortex, not delivered by the speakers. That is a literal answer to the jury's "give me a room, not a mixer" — the "room" here is the listener's own perception, and the piece is *about* the gap between what is played and what is heard. It is also the cheapest thing to review the jury keeps asking for: it only works on **headphones**, so it forces the sound-on review that is the standing bottleneck (jury #5). Rule-10-clean: 100% of the audible sound is Karel's real take — only a `StereoPanner` (temporal split) or a band-filter pair (spectral split) is automated; zero synthesis.

**Could become a prototype that does X:** raced this cycle (DEEP) as **`14944-phantomline`** (WINNER — note-gated pitch-height dichotic panner, the literal Deutsch condition, inline-SVG three-lane physical-vs-phantom score) vs `14960-earsplit` (spectral fission — the same take torn into complementary frequency bands across the ears, crossover + ear-assignment rotating, Canvas2D split-field; banked IDEAS §1174). Both lab-firsts (0× dichotic/illusion). Case a — today's research → today's build.

## 2026-08-18 (cycle 1173) — The score writes itself: animated TYPE as the music's own visualization

**Source:** *Visual Lyrics — Generating Animated Text for Music Lyric Videos with an Augmented Text Editor* (**ACM IUI 2026**, the 31st Intl. Conf. on Intelligent User Interfaces, dl.acm.org/doi/full/10.1145/3742413.3789072 — current-year, surfaced this cycle). The system generates a visually cohesive sequence of expressive **kinetic-text** effects timed to a song end-to-end: the author describes how letters behave *on the beat* and the backdrop they sit over, and the tool animates the type so the rhythm of the *type itself* — not a separate visualizer — carries the music. Lineage: **Norman McLaren's *Synchromy* (1971)**, music rendered as visible optical marks on the film strip `[older, foundational]`.

**Why it's the surprising move / the reframe.** The lab's ~1,100 pieces are almost entirely *fields* — shaders, particles, geometry, glow. Type is used only as chrome (labels, readouts), never as the art. IUI-2026 (and the broader 2026 kinetic-typography-lyric-video wave) treats **the words as the visualization**: weight, blur, scale, and position of letters ARE the audio-reactive surface. For a piano catalog with no lyrics, the "words" are the *score's own vocabulary* — chord symbols, section names, note-names — so the piece becomes a **self-writing score**: the music annotating itself in living type, the hero chord breathing on the RMS, the melody scattering its note-names at the edges. It is the sharpest possible answer to the jury 2026-08-17 provocation #4 ("commit a cycle to the register that's actually rare — a deliberately text-only / no-color piece"): a register the lab has literally never shipped.

**Could become a prototype that (this cycle):** ships the banked `14928-livescore` (WIDE §1172) as its own SHIP-BANKED cycle — Karel's real take through one `createSafeMaster`; the entire image is Geist type on near-black + violet; the current chord is a large word that re-writes itself (Web-Animations blur/scale) on every change and breathes with `analyser` RMS (font-weight 220–860 + scale); a section caption advances over the timeline; melody note-names ghost at the frame edges; a mono key/tempo/meter/dB/time readout; spectral-descriptor fallback (BRIGHT/WARM/DARK) if analysis is null. **→ `14928-livescore` (case c — a recent-research IDEAS entry [§1172 WIDE brief] re-chained to today's IUI-2026 dive on the same technique).** Freshness flagged honestly: the *concept* was authored last cycle; today's dive confirms it sits on a live 2026 research/tools front (animated-text-as-music), not a training-data curio.

## 2026-08-17 (cycle 1172) — Physics-driven visual-acoustic mapping: the *shape* of a chord as a standing wave

**Source:** *ChladniSonify — A Visual-Acoustic Mapping Method for Chladni Patterns in New Media Art Creation* (arXiv 2605.09846, 2026 — current-year cs-media listing surfaced this cycle). Alongside it, current-2026 tool activity around the same physics: CymaVis (interactive Chladni + 3D water-surface simulator) and Cymatica (Metal-GPU Chladni sim, up to 100k sand particles at 60–120fps) — evidence the "physics-driven audio-visual system" is a live 2026 research/tools hotspot, not a museum curio. Lineage: **Ernst Chladni's plate experiments (1787)** and **Nigel Stanford's *Cymatics* (2014)** `[older, foundational]`.

**Why it's the surprising move / the reframe.** The lab has FFT-bars, spectral clouds, particle-life, reaction-diffusion — but almost every audio→visual mapping is *statistical* (energy in a band → brightness/height/count). Chladni is *structural*: the pattern is the literal **standing-wave geometry** of the driving frequency on a constrained plate — nodal lines where the surface stays still, sand migrating to them. So the image is not "a picture of the loudness," it's "the physical SHAPE the sound would take if it were vibrating a plate." Driving the plate mode `(m,n)` from Karel's live *harmony* (chord root/quality + spectral peaks via `trackAnalysis`) makes the figure genuinely *read* the composition: a new chord → a new nodal figure crystallizes. It's a not-a-mixer verb (reveal/see, not conduct) and a fresh mapping *category* even though cymatics has appeared in the lab before — the novelty here is **harmony→mode** rather than raw-frequency→mode.

**Could become a prototype that (this cycle, WIDE fan-out):** thousands of Canvas2D sand particles descend the gradient of the square-plate nodal function `f(x,y)=cos(nπx)cos(mπy)−cos(mπx)cos(nπy)`, with plate mode interpolated from the current chord and jitter scaled by loudness (louder = "shake the plate harder" = scatter before settling), so the figure morphs as his harmony moves. **→ `14896-chladnichord` (case a, today→today).** Raced WIDE against two divergent non-mixer verbs: `14912-chordlattice` (his chords walk the neo-Riemannian **Tonnetz** as a glowing path — inline-SVG harmonic geometry) and `14928-livescore` (a self-writing **kinetic-typography** score — the music annotates itself in living type, the jury's "deliberately text-only / no-color" register). Non-winners banked to IDEAS §1172.

## 2026-08-17 (cycle 1171) — The music that lives *between* networked listeners: relational co-presence as the instrument

**Source / lineage:** the networked-music / telematic-performance lineage — **The Hub** and its predecessor the **League of Automatic Music Composers** (San Francisco, late 1970s–80s), the first computer-network band, whose members wired their machines to each other so that no single player produced the music: it emerged *in the wire between the nodes*. Framed by contemporary distributed-instrument / shared-listening-room writing and the standing observation (jury 2026-08-17 provocation #2) that the lab has shipped **zero genuinely multi-user pieces across 15+ cycles**. `[lineage older, foundational — the freshness here is the *mechanism*, not a <30-day paper]` — flagged honestly: the current-quarter cs.SD/cs.HC listings surface plenty of solo-interaction and single-agent-accompaniment systems, but the genuinely under-explored move for *this* lab is not another new DSP technique — it's the **social topology** of the instrument.

**Why it's the surprising move / the reframe.** Nearly all of the lab's 1,100+ pieces (and every recent standout — holdlight, disintegration, nave, answerback, choirglass) are **single-listener**: one person acts, the sound responds. Even the "not-a-mixer" wins are still *you and the machine*. The Hub inverts the social contract one level deeper than attention-gating did: **the composition is the relationship between two people**, and neither one holds it alone. The sharpest, cheapest expression is *distance as harmony* — two presences in a shared field whose separation IS the musical parameter: far = tension/detune, near = consonance + a third "between" layer that exists only when they meet. It answers the jury's headline "give me a room, not a mixer" literally: the room is the space *between two listeners*. Server-less **`BroadcastChannel`** makes it genuinely demoable (two tabs, no infra), and a drifting **ghost-partner** fallback keeps a solo morning-review whole.

**Could become a prototype that does X (this cycle → `14864-betweenus`, case a):** two browser contexts each loop a distinct real take; normalized field-distance `d` drives the second voice's `detune` from a wide spread (far) into a just major third + a rising convolution "between-bloom" (near); BroadcastChannel syncs positions/heartbeats with a Lissajous ghost fallback. Adjacent unbuilt hooks banked IDEAS §1171: **granular confluence** (proximity braids the two takes into one grain texture — the `14880-conflux` sibling), **negotiated consonance** (the pair settles on *which* interval by how they approach), and **true two-device presence** via a tiny WebRTC/WebSocket signaling relay (the still-open cross-device lane).

## 2026-08-17 (cycle 1170) — Attention itself as the primary creative tool: the listener as "composer-performer"

**Source:** *Exploring AI Audio Models in Soundwalking with Broader Audiences* — **Proceedings of the 2026 ACM Conference on Creativity & Cognition** (dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3803784.3816887), surfaced alongside the current-year installation-practice writing framing "attention itself as the primary creative tool" and the listener shifted "from a passive observer to a *composer-performer*" whose active participation is what constitutes the soundscape. Lineage/framing: **Pauline Oliveros — *Deep Listening*** (`[older, foundational]` — listening as an active, effortful, whole-body discipline, not passive reception). Freshness: the C&C proceedings are 2026 (current-year conference); I'm citing the *effortful-attention-as-authorship* thesis it shares with the soundwalking literature and flagging the exact publication date honestly rather than pinning it to a <30-day window.

**Why it's surprising / the reframe.** Nearly every one of the lab's 1,100+ pieces is *set-and-forget*: press start (or drop your body/voice/tilt in as a controller) and the sound persists on its own while you watch. Even the anti-mixer standouts still *give* you the sound. Soundwalking / Deep Listening invert the social contract at the root: **sound is not delivered to you; it exists only for as long as you actively spend attention to sustain it.** The moment you look away it's gone. That makes *attention a scarce, spendable material* — the composer-performer isn't conducting a mix, they're deciding, second by second and with a finite budget, what gets to exist. It's the sharpest possible answer to the jury's standing "give me a piece that isn't a catalog mixer at all."

**Could become a prototype that:** presents a few of Karel's real takes as filaments you can only keep sounding by *pressing and holding* — release and both sound and light decay; a hard cap of two at once (finite attention); and a *memory of neglect* so threads left untended dim, thin, revive slower, and can be lost for the session. **→ built this cycle as `14848-holdlight` (case-a, today's research → today's build).** Adjacent unbuilt hooks: an idle/gaze-timeout that quietly reclaims a held thread if your attention wanders (webcam presence as a *secondary* layer over catalog audio); a two-person version where sustaining a shared thread needs both listeners holding at once (the still-0× multi-user lane).

## 2026-08-17 (cycle 1169) — Nothing in the lab AGES: real-time controllable audio disintegration as a time-axis composer

**Source:** *False Memory* — real-time audio-disintegration effect (All the Machines; **Synthtopia, 2026-07-30**, ~18 days old / genuinely <30d): analog tape-degradation + digital corruption in a parallel signal path, headline feature an **"Evolve" knob that auto-ramps degradation over a user-set 1–64 minute window.** Lineage/framing: **William Basinski — *The Disintegration Loops* (2002)** (`[older, foundational]` — tape literally shedding oxide with each pass, the decay IS the composition) and arXiv:2511.17404 *"The Artist is Present"* (whether text-to-audio models reproduce artist traits like tape-degradation/loop decay). **Why it's surprising for us:** nearly every one of the lab's 1,100+ pieces reacts frame-to-frame and is *temporally stable* — none of them **age**. Decay-as-time-axis inverts that: the content is the *erosion itself*, legible only if you sit with it for minutes — the long-form-with-state/memory lane the jury named thin (only tidalstrata filled it). **Could become a prototype that:** takes ONE of Karel's real takes and lets it wear away over a set Evolve duration (creeping lowpass + amplitude dropouts + wow/flutter + a surfacing hiss/room-tone bed), rendered as a silver-gelatin emulsion that visibly degrades via WebGL2 feedback — audibly different at minute 5 than minute 1. **→ built this cycle as `14832-disintegration` (case-a, today's research → today's build).**

## 2026-08-17 (cycle 1168) — A recording is not an object, it is the excitation of an architecture: real-time geometric room acoustics for a "room, not a mixer"

**Source:** *RoomAcoustiC++ — An open-source room acoustic model for real-time audio simulations* (2026; researchgate.net/publication/400806636). Framing/lineage: *Past, Present, and Future of Spatial Audio and Room Acoustics* (arXiv:2503.12948, 2025-03) and the foundational Allen & Berkley **image-source method** (1979). Freshness caveat: RoomAcoustiC++ surfaced as a 2026 release but I could not pin it to a <30-day date — I'm citing it as the current state-of-the-art open engine and flagging the date honestly rather than dressing it as brand-new. The image-source method itself is `[older, foundational]`.

**Why it's surprising / the reframe.** RoomAcoustiC++ is a *hybrid*: geometric acoustics (an image-edge model that locates geometry-dependent early specular reflections + edge diffraction) for the early field, and a **feedback delay network** for the late reverberation — with a *moving* listener/source and binaural HRTF over headphones. The reframe for us: the lab's last six winners are all "controller-X drives the 16-track fader bank" (the jury's headline rut, 2026-08-17). Room acoustics inverts that social contract — there is **one** recording, and *moving the listener through modeled geometry re-renders the same take as a different acoustic experience*. You don't mix it; you inhabit it. Early reflections are just delayed/attenuated/panned mirror-image copies of the one source (cheap in Web Audio: DelayNode + GainNode + PannerNode per image); the diffuse tail is an FDN or a synthesized-IR ConvolverNode. It is rule-10-native (audible sound stays his real piano; the acoustics are a *model applied to* his audio, not synth music) and it is the most literal possible answer to Karel's "give me a room, not a mixer."

**Could become a prototype that:** places one of Karel's recordings inside a navigable virtual architecture where the acoustics change with your position — chained directly into this cycle's DEEP fan-out (case a): three rooms, three acoustic engines. `14768-afterglow` (NDE tunnel-to-light, depth-crossfaded convolution IRs, grayscale→white), `14784-nave` (walkable cathedral, true image-source early reflections + FDN tail, full-chromatic stained glass), `14800-sittingroom` (Lucier "I Am Sitting in a Room" iterative modal-resonance feedback, violet dissociative void). Adjacent unbuilt hooks: edge-diffraction "sound bends around a pillar" study; a multi-user shared acoustic room (WebRTC, the still-0× lane); depth-camera listener tracking as the locomotion input.

## 2026-08-17 (cycle 1167) — Audio illusions are perceptually machine-robust *because* they exploit human-only binding

**Source:** *Robust CAPTCHA Using Audio Illusions in the Era of Large Language Models* — arXiv:2601.08516 (2026-01); alongside **PESTO: Real-Time Pitch Estimation with Self-supervised Transposition-equivariant Objective** — arXiv:2508.01488 (2025-08). Freshness caveat: the CAPTCHA paper is ~7 months old, not <30 days — a genuinely <30-day cs.SD hit on this exact niche didn't surface; I'm citing it as the conceptual seed and flagging the date honestly rather than dressing an older source as fresh.

**Why it's surprising:** the CAPTCHA work weaponizes the fact that classic auditory illusions (Shepard–Risset endless glissando, missing-fundamental, Deutsch streaming) survive machine transcription *precisely because* they are artifacts of human perceptual binding — the ear/brain fuses cues an ASR pipeline reads literally. That reframes an illusion not as a novelty trick but as a piece of the listener: the endlessness only exists in *your* head. PESTO shows real-time, low-latency pitch tracking is now cheap enough to close the loop live.

**Could become a prototype that:** renders a Shepard–Risset endless glissando *from Karel's own piano timbre* — octave-stacked, pitch-shifted copies of a real take under a fixed raised-cosine spectral window, gliding forever — so the "infinite fall" is built entirely from his recording and completed only by the listener's ear. Chained directly into this cycle's build `14720-endlessreturn` (case a). Adjacent unbuilt hooks: a Deutsch scale-illusion stereo-streaming piece from his melody; a Risset *rhythm* (endless accelerando) of his tempo.

Agent appends findings here during research cycles. Each entry: date, source, 2-3 sentence summary, prototype speculation.

## §1166 — 2026-08-17 · Co-creation agents that "listen" hierarchically → a voice-led choir of your own catalog
Source: **"Helping Music Co-Creation Agents 'Listen' Well: Hierarchical Self-Supervised World Models," arXiv:2608.04378** (from arXiv `cs.SD/current`, Aug 2026). Secondary in the same listing: **arXiv:2608.03419** *Multi-Task Multi-Frame Visual Piano Transcription* (a keyboard's visual state encodes music — the inverse arrow) and the still-standing **arXiv:2608.03040** *Calliphony* (banked §1165 as strokesong).

**Why it's surprising / the hook.** Most interactive music systems — and most of this lab's 1,100+ pieces — react *frame-to-frame*: an FFT bin nudges a pixel, a fader nudges a gain. This paper argues a co-creation agent should hold a **hierarchical listening memory** (a world-model of the music at multiple timescales) and shape its response at the phrase/section level, not just the sample level. The reframing for us: the machine shouldn't merely *visualize* or *transform* a take — it should **listen to what the human is doing right now and answer musically from its memory of everything it knows**, which for Resonance is Karel's whole 16-track catalog.

**Could become a prototype that:** lets Karel **sing/hum** (mic = control-only, rule-10-safe — audible sound stays his real recordings) and answers by pulling, from across the whole catalog, the moments that *consonate* with his voice — his recorded self becoming a live choir tuned to him. Built this cycle as **SIREN** (DEEP, 2 engines raced): granular YIN pitch-matching (`14688-sirenchoir`, banked) vs. chroma-harmonic whole-take swell in a stained-glass rose window (`14704-choirglass`, **shipped**). Chain research→build = case (a): the winner IS the direct implementation of "the machine listens and answers harmonically from its corpus memory." Pulled also by Karel's love of `1326-voice-cathedral` (voice direction endorsed).

## §1165 — 2026-08-17 · Gesture as an EXPRESSIVE controller, not a trigger — calligraphy kinetics drive real-time music
Source: **Calliphony — "A Calligraphy-Driven Interface for Real-Time Generative Music Performance," arXiv:2608.03040** (from arXiv `cs.SD/current`, Aug 2026). The system turns *handwriting/brush gesture* into a continuous expressive musical controller: a stroke's speed, curvature and pressure shape musical expression in real time — not discrete note triggers, but a continuous "how it sounds right now" surface, closer to conducting than to a keyboard. Surprising because most gesture-music work maps position→pitch or taps→onsets; Calliphony maps the *kinetics of the mark itself* (its velocity envelope, its turns, its pressure) to expression, which is a genuinely different, more calligraphic verb. Secondary finds same listing: **Agogic (arXiv:2608.03999)** — "performance-timed music tokens," expressive micro-timing (agogics) as first-class symbolic tokens for LLM-native symbolic music; **MI-MIDI (arXiv:2608.06638)** — mechanistic-interpretability probing/steering of text-to-MIDI models. Could become a prototype that: **you WRITE music as calligraphy — brush-stroke kinetics continuously re-voice/conduct one of Karel's real recordings (speed→brightness/energy, curvature→spectral tilt, pressure→dynamics), the recording always playing underneath, the stroke shaping its expression.** → this cycle's **Brief A `14640-strokesong`** implements exactly this (adapted from synthesis to re-voicing a real take, per rule 10). Agogic is a future seed for a micro-timing / rubato-aware re-performance piece; MI-MIDI is a research-only curiosity (no direct AV hook).

## §1164 — 2026-08-17 · Swarm sonification: let an emergent, self-organizing field *arrange itself* — the system plays you, you don't play it

**Sources (dates verified).** (1) Sachin Sharma, *"Beyond WebGL: Real-Time Fluid Simulations Using WebGPU Compute Shaders"* (`sachinsharma.dev/blogs/webgpu-compute-shaders-fluid-dynamics-2026`, 2026) — GP-GPU SPH + PIC/FLIP entirely in WebGPU compute, tens of thousands of particles at 60fps with a ping-pong buffer + spatial-sort architecture. (2) Robert Borghesi, *ASTRODITHER* (2026) — audio-reactive three.js **WebGPU/TSL** fluid + bloom + dithered signal-noise, a live example of coupling audio to a GPU fluid. (3) *Swarm Sonification* (Jack N. Goodwin, `github.com/JackNGoodwin/Swarm-Sonification`) — an emergent additive/subtractive synthesiser that IS a Reynolds **Boids** (1987) flocking sim: the swarm's own local structure generates the sound, no external score. Lineage: Craig Reynolds *Boids* 1987 · Blackwell/Young *Swarm Granulator* (sound-art swarm aesthetics).

**Why it's surprising for us.** The lab's last FOUR winners — strainsong (physics→mix), bodychoir (body→mix), orbitcompass (heading→mix), skyflock (sun→mix) — are all *"a controller conducts the 16-track mix."* That's the successor rut to the single-recording-DSP-toy: the human is always the conductor. Swarm sonification inverts the social contract: **the arrangement emerges from a self-organizing field's own dynamics — you are immersed in it, not operating it.** It's cosmic-ambient/autonomous (the AGENT.md PRIMARY DIRECTION, a pole the 12/15-"perform" window has starved) AND rule-10-native: bind each of the 16 recordings to a *species*/cluster in the field, and let the field's emergent structure (which clusters cohere, split, migrate) decide which recordings sound and where they sit in space. No synth — the field is only a *mixer/spatialiser* over his real takes.

**Could become a prototype that:** renders his whole catalog as a **living emergent cosmos** — a GPU field of thousands of agents in 16 species, one per recording; a species that coheres into a tight fast cluster surges that track (louder, brighter, its centroid azimuth sets the pan), a species that scatters thins to silence; the cosmos runs itself (autonomous, boundless, installation-grade) with an OPTIONAL mic-secondary "breath/hum stirs the field" perturbation (control-only, never heard — rule 10 preserved). Deep-space **bioluminescent teal/aqua/cyan** (off both the banned warm AND the banned cool-violet). Chain **research → build** = case (a): this cycle's DEEP winner IS the direct implementation.

## §1163 — 2026-08-17 · Physics-based sonification: let the deformation of a material be the score

**Source:** the ISMIR-2026 / cs.SD sonification cluster surfaced this dive — **BioSonix** ("Can Physics-Based Sonification Perceptualize Tissue Deformations From Tool Interactions?", Ruozzi et al., arXiv 2026 window) sonifies the *strain field* of a deforming physical material as you push a tool into it — the sound IS the physics, moment to moment. Paired with **"Tonal Cognition in Sonification: Exploring the Needs of Practitioners in Sonic Interaction Design"** (arXiv:2408.17012), which argues sonification lands better when the mapping respects *tonal/harmonic cognition* rather than raw parameter-to-pitch. Corroborating the broader 2026 spatial/interaction-sonification line: ISMIR-2026 spatial-audio papers (binaural source-separation, in-the-wild spatialization arXiv:2506.00927).

**Why it's surprising / fresh:** almost every audio-reactive piece runs the arrow *audio → visual* (FFT drives pixels). BioSonix inverts it — a **physical simulation is the controller** and its continuous *deformation/strain* is the thing you hear. For a lab that has done 1,118 "sound drives the picture" pieces, "the picture (a physics sim) drives the sound" is the genuinely under-explored direction, and the tonal-cognition paper says: don't map strain to raw pitch, map it to *which already-musical material swells* — which is exactly rule-10 (his catalog is the tonal material; the sim only mixes it).

**Could become a prototype that does X:** a soft-body / mass-spring membrane of Karel's catalog that you **tip under gravity** (phone tilt) — local strain (tension bright/loud, compression dark/quiet) voices each region's recordings, so a physical material's deformation conducts the catalog with zero synthesis. → seeded this cycle's WIDE brief `14512-strainsong` (the direct research→build implementation); raced against two divergent explorers — `14528-seismosong` (the planet's live USGS earthquakes as the arranging data-field) and `14544-humchoir` (your voice as a secondary call-and-response control over a catalog choir). Winner cited in STATE §1163.

## §1162 — 2026-08-17 · Gesture-as-ghost: the touchless instrument's second act is layering yourself

**Source:** Theremin "Ghost Hands" MR add-on, Meta Quest — launched **2026-07-01** (Upload VR Showcase 2026; uploadvr.com, "Theremin's Ghost Hands Add-On Lets You Be A One Person Musical Ensemble This July"). Corroborating, browser-native and even fresher: **Gesture Synth** (synthgesture.com), a webcam hand-gesture instrument **updated 2026-08-04**.

**Why it's surprising / fresh:** the touchless-gesture-instrument lineage (Léon Theremin's 1920 aetherphone) crossed a threshold this past month — glove-free hand/body tracking is now good enough that the interesting problem is no longer "map a gesture to a pitch," it's **the memory of the gesture**: Ghost Hands records a motion loop and replays it as a *cloned performer*, so one player becomes an ensemble. The theremin's second act isn't the gesture — it's laying gesture on gesture. Pairs with §1152's beat-locked layering and the loved `172-loop-station`.

**Could become a prototype that does X:** conduct/mix Karel's WHOLE 16-track catalog with the bare body in the air (webcam MediaPipe landmarks) — raise/spread arms to swell and open halves of the catalog, lean to tilt the spectrum — then RECORD a conducting pass so a translucent "ghost body" holds that mix while you layer another. Rule-10-clean: the body never synthesizes a tone; it only mixes/shapes his real recordings (0 synth). Cashes jury #1 (break the cool-violet monoculture → full 16-hue chromatic spectrum) + the input-diversity gap (camera/body, off the pointer≈9× / keyboard≈4× rut). → shipped this cycle as `14480-bodychoir`, DEEP-winner of a 3-approach race (losers: hands-dome-of-orbs `14464-ghostmix` + hands-gravity-well-nebula `14496-handnebula`, both fully built demoable, banked IDEAS §1162, `rm -rf`'d pre-build).

## §1161 — 2026-08-17 · Sonify a 3D scene as a 360° RING (each azimuth = a sound cue) — and the screen-bias inversion

15-min dive (WebSearch, dates verified). **Primary hook — spatial sonification as a ring.** *"A Scene Representation for Online Spatial Sonification"* (arXiv:2412.05486, Cheng Wang et al.) projects a 3D scene onto a **360° circular/cylindrical rasterisation** where each angular position maps to a specific sound cue — a minimalist geometry that turns "what is around me" into an intuitive audio field. Paired with the 2026 spatial-audio-generation line — *"Towards Streaming Synchronized Spatial Audio Generation via Autoregressive Diffusion Transformer"* (arXiv:2605.30940, May 2026) and *Sonic4D* (arXiv:2506.15759) — which frame spatial audio as a *streaming field around a listener*, not a fixed stereo mix. **The inverse is the instrument for us:** don't sonify a scene — place Karel's 16 real recordings as fixed points on a ring and let the *listener's heading* rasterise the ring into a live binaural mix (turn your body → change the mix). Surprise-for-us: the lab has spatialised recordings before (`13840-hallofsongs`, HRTF walk-among-takes) but never made **heading/compass** the instrument, and the last five ships are all Canvas2D — a ring in three.js breaks both ruts. → build (case a): `14400-orbitcompass`.

**Secondary finding — the screen-bias inversion (banked, not built this cycle).** The 2026 brainwave-entrainment literature (NIH-review summaries, Mind Alive 2026 guide) reports *audiovisual* entrainment tends to beat audio-only because the visual cortex responds faster — yet slow **resonance-paced breathing (~5.5 breaths/min)** reliably drives parasympathetic calm. The provocation: deliberately **invert** the lab's screen-first habit — a near-*screenless* piece (one slow breath-glow) carried by rich moving spatial audio, breath as the clock. Could become a prototype that plays the catalog as a slow orbiting field you experience *eyes-closed* — banked as `14416-stillpoint` (IDEAS §1161). **Safety:** any such piece keeps luminance to slow multi-second drift (<0.3 Hz), never flicker.

## §1160 — 2026-08-16 · Long-form coherence = a TWO-TIMESCALE engine (slow global scaffold + fast local detail + memory)

15-min dive (WebSearch: "long-form generative music evolving state memory 2026 ambient composition arxiv", dates verified). The load-bearing 2026 finding is a diagnosis of *why* long-form generation drifts or loops: **"Fusing Memory and Attention: A study on LSTM, Transformer and Hybrid Architectures for Symbolic Music Generation" (arXiv:2603.21282, Expert Systems with Applications 2026)** compares the two families on 17 musical-quality metrics and finds them complementary — **LSTM/recurrent memory captures local melodic continuity but *fails to preserve long-range dependencies*, while attention/Transformers model global structure but not moment-to-moment feel; the fix is a hybrid that runs BOTH.** Paired with **"Efficient Long-Sequence Diffusion Modeling for Symbolic Music Generation" (arXiv:2603.00576, 2026)** — a structured **state-space model** carries long-range context at near-linear cost while a separate refinement pass fixes *local* detail: the same slow-scaffold / fast-detail split, in a different architecture. Both descend, conceptually, from **Brian Eno's *Reflection* (2017)** — a generative system audibly different minutes later yet coherent throughout. **Why it's surprising for us:** the lab has shipped 1,159 prototypes and has **no true long-form flagship** — almost everything is a loop or a 1–3 minute toy; the jury's own diversity menu flags "long-form generative (5+ min with state, memory, evolution)" as a category that "hasn't appeared." The papers hand us the *architecture* to build one: don't try to compose a 10-minute arc with one process — split it into a SLOW form-scaffold (what region of the catalog / harmony / density is active, moving every 30–90s) riding over a FAST detail process (grain-to-grain or frame-to-frame continuity), plus a MEMORY that re-surfaces earlier material so minute 8 recalls minute 1. **Chained straight into today's DEEP build** (case a): the concept "your whole catalog as a ~10-min self-evolving piece with real memory," raced via two engines — granular concatenative (`14320-driftmass`) vs multi-stratum spectral crossfade (`14336-tidalstrata`, shipped). Sources: [Fusing Memory and Attention arXiv:2603.21282](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21282), [Efficient Long-Sequence Diffusion arXiv:2603.00576](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.00576), Brian Eno *Reflection* (2017, generative long-form).

## §1159 — 2026-08-16 · MIDI-driven concatenative resynthesis: play a live instrument out of your OWN recordings

15-min dive (WebSearch: "Web MIDI concatenative synthesis live performance 2026 browser generative music"). The strongest thread is **concatenative MIDI-to-audio**: **CoSaRef — "Annotation-Free MIDI-to-Audio Synthesis via Concatenative Synthesis and Generative Refinement" (arXiv:2410.16785)** performs concatenative synthesis driven by a MIDI performance and refines it, requiring no aligned MIDI-audio pairs — i.e. **the played notes are voiced by re-assembling slices of an existing audio corpus at matching pitch.** Pair it with the running lab reference **The Concatenator (arXiv:2411.04366)** — real-time concatenative musaicing — and the framing this cycle is: *your MIDI keyboard, voiced entirely in grains of your own recorded piano, over one of your own takes.* The 2026 framing that makes it timely: **"A Design Space for Live Music Agents" (arXiv:2602.05064, Feb 2026)** — the design vocabulary for a performer playing *with* a responsive system in real time (call/response, accompaniment, timbre-transfer). Sources verify to 2024–2026; CoSaRef is the direct mechanism (concatenative MIDI→audio, tagged `[foundational, direct-mechanism]`), the live-agents paper is the current-year performance framing. **Why it's surprising for us:** the lab has **never** touched Web MIDI (0× across 1,158 cycles per JURY.md) even though Karel is a pianist — and concatenative resynthesis lets his live playing *sound like him* without a single oscillator. **Chained straight into today's WIDE build** (case a): `14256-mididuet` — Web MIDI (first in lab) → for each played note, pick a real slice of the catalog at that pitch (via `trackAnalysis.notes`), pitch-nudge & envelope it, play over a chosen bed take. He duets with himself, in his own timbre. Sources: [CoSaRef arXiv:2410.16785](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.16785), [The Concatenator arXiv:2411.04366](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.04366), [A Design Space for Live Music Agents arXiv:2602.05064](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.05064).

## §1158 — 2026-08-16 · graphic notation as a live COMPOSE surface, and the corpus as the sound bank (concatenative musaicing)

15-min dive (WebSearch, dates verified). Two threads converged. (1) **Graphic / animated notation is having a 2026 moment as an *interactive* medium, not just a printed art object.** The landmark reference *From Xenakis's UPIC to Graphic Notation Today* (ZKM / Hatje Cantz) went **open-access in 2025** (OAPEN, library.oapen.org/handle/20.500.12657/97868) with interactive QR-linked compositions; alongside it, live-score tools like `sign-e` (cdm.link/sign-e — interactive live graphic scores driving Ableton) and INScore (augmented, dynamic notation) show the score has become a real-time control surface. The lineage: Xenakis's UPIC (1977, draw a gesture → hear it) + Cornelius Cardew's *Treatise* (1967, 193 pages of abstract ink glyphs with no fixed sound-mapping). (2) **The corpus-as-sound-bank framing** — *The Concatenator: A Bayesian Approach to Real-Time Concatenative Musaicing* (arXiv:2411.04366) + the CataRT/SKataRT/CatOracle line — reconstruct a target by re-assembling slices of an existing collection by acoustic similarity. **Why it's surprising for us:** the lab has repeatedly *analyzed* and *rearranged* single takes, but never let Karel **compose a wholly new piece** by selecting-and-placing phrases from the *whole* catalog onto a notation surface, with harmony curating the result. **Chained straight into today's DEEP build** (case a + b — it also resurrects the ⭐⭐⭐⭐ §1157 `inkscore` seed): a paper-white ink graphic score you play at the keyboard, where each mark is a real phrase-slice and a Krumhansl key-profile engine keeps the woven result in key. Sources: [OAPEN UPIC book](https://library.oapen.org/handle/20.500.12657/97868), [The Concatenator arXiv:2411.04366](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.04366), [sign-e / CDM](https://cdm.link/sign-e/), Organised Sound "The Future is Graphic: Animated notation for contemporary practice."

## §1157 — 2026-08-16 · generative ambient in 2026 is "shaped by the world" — the piece is a function of your real environment, not a loop

**Source:** the 2026 world-responsive generative-ambient wave — **Sonaur** ("Ambient Sound Shaped by the World," sonaur.app, 2026 — generative ambient driven by real-time weather/location), the **March 2026 "Ambient Engine for macOS"** (Synthtopia, 2026-03-23 — on-device generative soundscape engine), and **Endel**'s time-of-day / biometric adaptive soundscapes — all descendants of **Brian Eno's *Reflection*** (2017, the generative app that modulates its output by the time of day; generativemusic.com). The shared lesson: the composition is no longer a fixed timeline or a loop — it's a *function* whose inputs are the real environment (sun, weather, hour, place), so the same system sounds different at dawn than at dusk, today than tomorrow. The classic **Circadian Machine** installation (plays by time of day, special actions at sunrise/sunset) is the physical-install precedent.
**Why it's surprising / relevant:** the fresh jury (2026-08-16) named two ruts — **cool-violet-on-near-black ~12×** and **the single-recording-DSP-toy template 7×** — and asked to treat the **whole catalog as one corpus**. A world-shaped generative arranger answers all three: it consumes ALL 16 of Karel's recordings as a corpus (not one take), its *palette is literally the sky* (full daylight spectrum, the opposite of cool-violet), and its input is the real sun/clock — a **real-world-data-sonification** input the lab has **never** used (every prior input is pointer/mic/MIDI/camera/tilt/WASD). It also re-opens the **cosmic-ambient / autonomous pole** that the recent 12/15-"perform" window has starved — an installation-mode candidate (care #5) that plays itself in a room all day.
**Could become a prototype that:** loads Karel's whole catalog and *arranges it by the real sky* — the sun's actual elevation/azimuth (NOAA solar-position math from device time + geolocation) drives which recordings are audible, their equal-power gains, and a slow evolving crossfade, so at dawn it's sparse and high, at noon full and bright, at dusk warm, at deep night minimal — over a Canvas2D full-daylight-spectrum sky with a light-mote flock tracking the mix. → **shipped this cycle as `14224-skyflock` (WIDE winner; losers `14240-inkscore` paper-white keyboard-composed graphic score + `14256-tidecorpus` tilt-poured three.js corpus-water banked IDEAS §1157).**

## §1156 — 2026-08-16 · MIDI-to-audio in 2026 is timbre-BY-CORPUS — the played sound is the samples you pick, not a synth

**Source:** the 2026 controllable MIDI-to-audio synthesis line — **CoSaRef, "Annotation-Free MIDI-to-Audio Synthesis via Concatenative Synthesis and Generative Refinement" (arXiv:2410.16785**, actively cited 2025–26), **MIDI-VALLE** (neural-codec expressive-piano synthesis conditioned on a reference recording), and the surrounding DDSP / timbre-transfer family. They share one lesson: the played timbre is set by the **source audio you condition on / concatenate from**, not by an oscillator. CoSaRef in particular does plain concatenative synthesis from a chosen sample set FIRST, then optionally refines — so the browser-feasible CORE (pick real slices to match the MIDI, concatenate, tune) is exactly the rule-10 move: use ONE of Karel's takes as the corpus and let his live MIDI select which of his own notes/grains sound.
**Why it's surprising / relevant:** the lab's `13696-callback` already proved concatenative grains of his sound (QWERTY call-and-response, 2/5), but the fresh jury (2026-08-16) flagged **MIDI as 0×** and the palette as a **cool-violet monoculture (~12×)**. The CoSaRef frame says the timbre engine is a solved, browser-native problem once you accept concatenation without neural refinement — so the whole build budget can go to the two things the jury actually wants: **his MIDI keyboard as the primary verb**, and a **genuinely new color register**. It also validates a second, PURER engine callback never tried: index the recording's discrete **note-roll** (from `loadTrackAnalysis`) by pitch and replay his ACTUAL struck notes (real attack + decay), not grains — CoSaRef's "unit = one real recorded note" limit case, and the purest possible rule-10 grounding.
**Could become a prototype that:** plays one of his takes as a soft bed and lets him improvise OVER it on a MIDI keyboard (QWERTY fallback), where every key sounds his own nearest-pitch recorded note sliced live from the take and tuned to land in pitch — a full-chromatic **Scriabin *clavier à lumières*** color organ (each pitch class its own hue, C=red, circle-of-fifths) blooming under his hands, no synth anywhere. → **shipped this cycle as `14192-notemirror` (DEEP; the concatenative-grain sibling `14176-handduet` banked IDEAS §1156).**

## §1155 — 2026-08-16 · Browser-collaborative music in 2026 is CONTROL-broadcast, not audio-broadcast — the shared clock is the instrument

**Source:** the 2026 real-time collaborative-music web stack — Cornelljam / online-sequencer / Intersymmetric-Sequencer and the surrounding "remote jam" tooling all reach sub-50ms multiplayer the same way: they **broadcast compact control + clock events** (note-ons, transport ticks, a shared tempo epoch) over WebRTC data channels / WebSockets — or, same-origin, over `BroadcastChannel` — and **never stream the audio itself**. Anything above ~50ms round-trip feels unresponsive, so the winning move is to keep audio local and only sync the timeline. (Also in-window: gesture-driven real-time control — *Gesture2Music* arXiv:2511.00793, virtual-orchestra conducting arXiv:2604.27957 — same lesson: send the control signal, render locally.)
**Why it's surprising / relevant:** the lab already built the hard part and wasted it. `11888-chorusrooms` shipped a genuinely novel **server-less BroadcastChannel leader-elected shared clock** — real multi-tab ensemble, the thinnest category in the lab — but drove it with **oscillator synth**, a rule-10 miss the concept jury flagged explicitly (provocation #4: "resurrect chorusrooms with his catalog"). The 2026 control-broadcast pattern is exactly the frame that turns that infrastructure into a rule-10 instrument: keep his decoded recording local in every tab, sync only the leader's beat clock, and each tab becomes one voice.
**Could become a prototype that:** makes every open tab / device one voice in a **canon of Karel's own recorded piano** — a leader-elected `BroadcastChannel` clock keeps all voices phase-locked, each enters after an assignable delay in bars (a staggered round), and you conduct the ensemble by opening tabs, assigning tracks, and changing entry delays live; a single tab auto-spawns virtual voices so it's never empty. → **explored this cycle (WIDE) as `14128-choirtabs` (research-chained, cashes jury #4).**

## §1154 — 2026-08-16 · From a FLAT recurrence map to a HIERARCHICAL, beat-locked one — the 2026 music-structure-analysis turn

**Source:** the current MIR structure-analysis line — self-supervised **hierarchical metrical-structure modeling** (an 8-layer beat→measure→section tree trained on beat-aligned signals), **EDMFormer** (genre-specific self-supervised section segmentation, arXiv Mar 2026), and the **unsupervised evaluation of deep audio embeddings for MSA** (bar-wise embeddings segmented into labeled sections, arXiv Mar 2026) — all sitting on the foundational **Jonathan Foote, "Automatic Audio Segmentation Using a Measure of Audio Novelty," IEEE ICME 2000** (the checkerboard-kernel novelty curve that turns a self-similarity matrix into section boundaries).
**Why it's surprising / relevant:** cycle 1153's `14048-rhymeloom` shipped the *flat* version — fixed-length frames, one similarity matrix, leaps that could land mid-phrase because nothing was beat- or section-aware. The whole 2026 research turn is the opposite direction: structure is **hierarchical and beat-synchronous** — features per beat, bars grouped into sections, sections into form. Two cheap, fully browser-native pieces close rhymeloom's honest gap: (1) **beat/downbeat tracking** (spectral-flux novelty → autocorrelation tempo → phase-aligned 4/4 grid, per Ellis DP-2007 & Bello-2005, already proven in `13968-flipdeck`) so every leap snaps to a downbeat and lands *in time*; (2) **Foote checkerboard novelty segmentation** so the song is carved into its actual sections (A/B/C…) you can loop, solo, and leap between — the map becomes hierarchical, not a flat grid.
**Could become a prototype that:** takes rhymeloom's beat-synchronous chroma self-similarity, runs Foote-2000 novelty peak-picking to detect real section boundaries + clusters returning themes into labeled sections, and lets you PLAY the form — loop a section, solo it, leap to a rhyming *downbeat*, or wander an endless in-time re-telling — every sound a slice of his own piano. → **shipped this cycle as `14096-songwheel` (case b: cycle-2 deepening of the rhymeloom lineage), a radial song-form wheel; the WebGL2-matrix and node-graph siblings banked (IDEAS §1154).**

## §1153 — 2026-08-16 · Music-structure analysis (self-similarity / recurrence) as a PLAYABLE surface, not a readout

**Source:** arXiv MIR line on music structure analysis via self-similarity — SSM-Net (feature learning with an SSM-based loss), the Correlation Block-Matching barwise segmentation algorithm (arXiv:2311.18604), and the 2025–2026 "Segment Transformer" structural-analysis work (arXiv:2509.08283) — all descend from Jonathan Foote's foundational *"Visualizing Music and Audio using Self-Similarity"* (ACM MM 1999). The recurrence structure of a piece — which bars rhyme with which — is a cheap, fully browser-native computation (chroma per beat → cosine self-similarity), no model or network needed.
**Why it's surprising / relevant:** the lab has repeatedly *drawn* structure (67-structure-viz, 777-song-architecture) as something you watch. The unexploited move is Paul Lamere's **"The Infinite Jukebox"** (2012): use the same beat-similarity matrix as a *navigation graph* — jump between beats that sound alike to play a song forever, always landing on a musically-matching moment. That turns Karel's fixed take into an instrument: the analysis IS the interface, and you perform by choosing which echo to leap to. Directly answers the standing jury #1 (interaction over analysis) with the most rigorous MIR technique in the lab.
**Could become a prototype that:** slices one real recording into beat-segments, computes a Goertzel-bank chroma + cosine self-similarity matrix, precomputes each segment's top-K rhymes, and lets you improvise an endless coherent path by clicking the matrix / auto-wandering / steering a coherence threshold — every sound a slice of his own piano, gapless equal-power joins so leaps never click. → **shipped this cycle as `14048-rhymeloom` (case a).**

## §1152 — 2026-08-16 · The 2026 turn is interactive real-time REMIX / "generative delay" — flip the record, don't just analyze it

**Sources (dates verified):** **Live Music Diffusion Models: Efficient Fine-Tuning and Post-Training of Interactive Diffusion Music Generators (arXiv:2605.22717, May 2026)** — repurposes an audio diffusion model into an interactive "generative delay" that transforms a musician's live improvisation for variable timbral effects, running locally on a consumer gaming laptop — + **LK_Jam (arXiv:2606.21018, Jun 2026)** — a real-time bidirectional human-AI interactive music generation system (lightweight role-aware GRU + lock-free audio host). Enabling classical MIR: **BeatNet+ (Heydari et al., real-time rhythm analysis, TISMIR)**, **D. Ellis "Beat Tracking by Dynamic Programming" (2007)**, **Bello et al. "A Tutorial on Onset Detection in Music Signals" (IEEE TSAP 2005)**.

**Why it's surprising / on-point:** the recent lab window has performed every verb on his recordings EXCEPT rhythm — un-mix, walk-the-catalog, conduct-with-body, spectral-freeze, duet, morph, canon all touch pitch/timbre/space; none touch **groove**. The 2026 systems above are all about *interactively re-timing/remixing a performance in real time* — the cleanest possible cash of JURY #1 (FIVE cycles: let Karel DO something). Full neural remix needs a model (infeasible under no-heavy-deps), but real-time **beat/downbeat tracking + bar-quantized re-scheduling** is cheap, deterministic, browser-native, and has **never shipped in the lab as a re-composition instrument** — `13248-rubatoline` ran onset-only analysis to *draw* his rubato (a diagram you watch), never a beat-quantized thing you PLAY. Loved slugs `172-loop-station` + `106-beat-cut` confirm the appetite.

**Could become a prototype that:** loads one of his real recordings, detects tempo/beats/downbeats live, and hands Karel a beat-locked rig to **flip his own piano into a new groove** — scrub/loop/reverse/stutter quantized to his bars so it always stays in time, in his own timbre. → built this cycle as `13968-flipdeck` (DEEP, case a; raced against `13952-flipgrid` pad-sampler + `13984-flowloom` generative beat-loom, both banked).
